## A concurrency hazard I hit and resolved

Running `build`/`typecheck`/`test`/`hygiene`/`lint`/`test:snapshot` concurrently in the background (several invoke their own `tsc -b`/`tsdown` against shared `lib/` outputs) produced two **transient, self-inflicted** failures that are not real bugs:
- `typecheck` once failed with `Cannot find entry: lib/types/{index,invariant,startup}.js` — a race left the root package's build output incomplete.
- `hygiene`'s `knip` once flagged `scripts/staged-lint-probe-*.ts` and `.rendered-model-*` fixture dirs as "unused files" — it caught another concurrently-running command's ephemeral temp files mid-existence.

Fixed by running `pnpm run clean` (removed 278 stale paths, including leftover `node_modules`-only shells under `packages/desktop/{tool-desktop,desktop,desktop-domain,surface-chat}/` — real source was already git-deleted, only pnpm's per-workspace symlink folders remained) and re-running `build`, then re-verifying `typecheck`/`hygiene`/`lint`/`test`/`test:snapshot`/`doc-sync` one at a time. This also fixed a real, rename-caused test failure: **`packages/core/tools/tests/gen-tool-catalog.spec.ts`** (6 tests) was failing because the stale `packages/desktop/tool-desktop` shell was still visible to the on-disk package scanner as `tool-desktop`; clean removed it and all 10 tests in that file now pass.

Restored the links to the real, existing filenames.

Plus mechanical regeneration via `gen-cordis-catalog`, `gen-cordis-api`, `gen-client-catalog`, `gen-cordis-inspect-catalog`, `gen-tool-catalog`, `gen-config-catalog`, `gen-doc-graphs`, `gen-persistence-catalog`, `gen-third-party-notices`, `gen-module-graph`, `gen-scoped-events`, which updated `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/capability-seams.md`, `docs/config-catalog.md`, `docs/event-producer-consumer.md`, `docs/module-graph.md`, `docs/subsystems/README.md(+.zh.md)`, `docs/tool-catalog.md`, `docs/web-styling.md(+.zh.md)`, and regenerated the `cordis-surface` region in both `docs/subsystems/space.md` and `.zh.md` (that generator mirrors non-prose signature/JSDoc content verbatim into both language files by design — not a hand-translation). 5. **Two files outside the four packages were edited** (`api/remotes` client type re-export, `personal-client-runner` bench fixture), plus the generated docs/catalog above. - `src/client/contract/space.ts` — `SpaceViewState.preset: SpacePresetDescriptor | null`; `ISpace` gains `presets()` and `createSpace(presetId)`.
- `src/client/manager.ts` — `installBaseline(resolved: ResolvedSpace)` stores the preset; new private `advance(snapshot, preset)` shared by `installBaseline`/`installUpdate` so a mutation reply keeps the installed preset; `installPresets()` republishes like `installSurfaces`; `markUnavailable()` carries the preset over and no longer re-publishes when already unavailable (identity-based dedupe would have broken with a spread).
- `src/client/service.ts` — `resolveBaseline` reads `result.value.space.id`; new `createBaseline`; `refreshSurfaces` reads `listSurfaces` and `listPresets` in parallel, each failure keeping the previous value; `startResolve` generalized to `startBaseline(read)`; `createSpace` awaits any in-flight baseline then takes the single-flight slot itself (the "join the resolve machinery" option), so a resolve of the replaced Space can neither overlap nor land after the create.
- `src/client/identity.ts` — new `replaceLocationSpaceId(spaceId)` (rewrites `?space=` only when present, via `history.replaceState`; degrades where there is no document location).
- `src/client/index.ts` — type-only passthrough of `SpacePresetDescriptor`/`SpacePresetId` so ui-shell names the vocabulary without importing `api-remotes`.

- `README.md` / `README.zh.md` + `verify-translation-pairing --write`.

**`packages/client/ui-shell`**
- `chrome.ts` — pure `offeredSurfaces(catalog, preset)` (`'all'` keeps everything, allowlist filters by `applicationId`, `null` offers nothing).
- `Taskbar.tsx` — `presets`/`launcher`/`onCreateSpace` props; launcher button and its `Menu` omitted entirely when `launcher` is false (rover offset keeps window buttons and keyboard traversal correct); menu gains a separator plus one `new-space:&lt;id&gt;` entry per preset (a Surface id can never carry a colon).
- `SpaceFrame.tsx` — computes `offeredSurfaces(surfaces, view.preset)` once, passes it to the Taskbar and uses it for the `SESSION_SURFACE_ID` check in `resolveOpen`; forwards `presets` and `onCreateSpace`.

- `npx tsc -b tsconfig.client.json` — clean (exit 0).

No `oxlint-disable` directive was removed; one type-aware false positive (`no-unnecessary-type-assertion` on `['notes' as ApplicationId]`, where the cast is genuinely required by tsc) was avoided by routing the allowlist through the `allowing()` helper rather than suppressing it.

## Deviations from the contract

1. **`packages/test-support/client-runtime` was edited** although it is not in my scope list (it is also not in the do-not-edit list). `TestSpace implements ISpace`, so widening `ISpace` breaks it at compile time and `pnpm run build` — a stated success criterion — cannot pass without it. Three files: `src/space.ts`, `src/fixtures.ts`, `tests/space.client.spec.ts`.
2. **`createSpace` concurrency** uses the "join the resolve machinery" alternative the contract allows, not a `mutationTail`-style chain: `startResolve` became `startBaseline(read)` and `createSpace` waits out any in-flight baseline before taking the slot. A `mutationTail` clone would have left a window in which a `space/changed`-triggered resolve of the old Space could land after the create installed.
3. **ui-shell coverage is not gated**: `packages/client/ui-shell/src/*` sits under the repo's existing GUI coverage exemption in `vitest.config.ts`, so a scoped `--coverage` run reports zero instrumented files there. space-runtime is at 100% per file.

- apps/web e2e goldens: I looked for movement from the launcher menu and found none. `space-window-management/taskbar.expected.md` and `space-narrow/narrow-taskbar.expected.md` capture only the toolbar's buttons (the launcher menu is portalled and never in them), and `openWindowFromLauncher` in `apps/web/tests/space-windows.ts` matches menu items with `exact: true`, so the new "New Developer space" rows do not collide. The verification lane should still re-check once a `shared`-preset composition exists, since a launcher-less Space changes the toolbar's button set.</result>

## New files

- `apps/web/tests/space-shared-preset.e2e.ts` — one Host, two pages, zero model calls. Page 1 lands on the default `developer` Space (asserts `presetId`), and the launcher menu lists exactly `Chat`, `Notes`, `Plugin Studio`, `New Developer space`, `New Shared space`. It creates the `shared` Space from that launcher; the page installs it, the launcher button is gone, no Window buttons, and the record shows `presetId: shared` with **no** Windows (the `initialSurface: chat` seed is outside the allowlist and is dropped). The Space is then arranged host-side through `ctx.spaces.openWindow` for `note` (twice — a full one and the cascaded floating one, so the guest has real chrome to press), after which Window buttons are present while the launcher stays absent. The typed refusal is pinned once at the Host seam: `openWindow` for `chat` returns `{status:'rejected', code:'application-unavailable', message:'space surface "chat" belongs to application "chat", which space preset "shared" does not offer'}` and opens nothing. A second page (`newEnglishPage` while page 1 stays open) at `?space=&lt;sharedId&gt;` draws both Notes Windows, has no launcher, and operates them — taskbar focus, chrome minimize, taskbar restore — with the Host record and page 1 converging on each change. A reload reconstructs the Space and keeps the launcher absent. Console tripwires assert clean on both pages.
- `apps/web/tests/space-application-outage.e2e.ts` + `apps/web/tests/space-application-outage.overlay.yml` — two Hosts over one harness home/storage root. Host 1 seeds a Host-only draft and arms a promotion with `target: {kind:'application', applicationId:'notes'}` and `exports: ['notesCompanion']`, approves it through `approvePromotion` (a candidate with no Client half commits in-process, so host 1 needs no browser and the file costs two boots, not three), and asserts `runStatus: 'running'` with null diagnostics. Host 2 boots with the overlay, which disables only the host `surface-note` row — the row that registers both the `notes` Application and the `note` Surface. ## Deviation you need to know about (item 4's premise was wrong)

The brief said a plugin targeted at an unregistered Application "commits but reports a named `application-mount` failure". The implementation does the opposite on the **promotion** path: `startApproval` starts the candidate *before* committing (`packages/plugin-runtime/plugin-registry/src/index.ts:913-919`), and a missing mount makes `startCandidate` fail, so `failPromotion` returns `status: 'failed'`, the request stays armed, and **nothing commits** — the existing `approve-promotions` fixture would have thrown on that decision, and there would be no durable record to assert. This is deliberate and pinned by `packages/plugin-runtime/plugin-registry/tests/installation-target.spec.ts:138-163`. The named failure that *does* survive as a durable record is the boot path (same spec, :165).

So I built the honest equivalent, covering both halves the plan's "Verification strategy" bullet asks for, as one install-then-restart pair (the plugin-promotion scenario's own shape):

- **`plugin-target`** (composition *with* the chat Application): `cordis_define` → `plugin_promote` with `target: "chat"` and `exports: ["chatStamp"]` → existing approve fixture commits → `plugin_list` shows **running**. Model-visible: `…would install package sha256:… on application chat, contributing surfaces: none`. Durable: `target: {kind:'application', applicationId:'chat'}`, `manifest.exports: ['chatStamp']`.
- **`plugin-target-restart`** (the existing `plugin-promotion.cordis.snapshot.yml`, which registers **no** Application, with the two roots carried over): boot records the failure by name, `plugin_list` shows **failed**, `plugin_inspect` shows `Last failure (application-mount): no application "chat" is registered here, so there is no mount to run this plugin under`, final model text relays it; the record stays `enabled` on the same target, and stderr is empty (boot does not warn).

Two secondary notes on that scenario: `plugin_list` renders only `runStatus`, never diagnostics, so the named message needed a `plugin_inspect` round to be model-visible; and I used the `chat` Application rather than `notes` because `@deepseek-ai/dsh-surface-note` is not in `examples/package.json` dependencies — naming `notes` would have meant editing that manifest and the lockfile, outside the given scope.

<result>All work is complete and verified. Final report:

## Per-file changes

**`packages/client/ui-primitives`**
- `src/Menu.tsx` — new tagged entry kind `MenuSubmenu = { type: 'submenu', id, label, items: readonly MenuItem[] }`; `MenuItem` gains `type?: never`, so a tagged entry (a submenu above all) is a **type error** inside a submenu's items — exactly one level, enforced by the compiler. The old untyped `MenuItem.submenu` field is gone (it had no product consumer, only tests). New `SubmenuRow` subcomponent portals the card to `document.body`, fixed-positioned from the row's rect (`rect.right + SUBMENU_GAP`, `rect.top - CARD_GAP`), re-placed on capture-phase scroll and resize, rendered hidden until placed like a portal list. Keyboard: one `onKeyDown` on the root wrapper (the React tree puts trigger, portaled list and portaled card all under it) handles ArrowDown/Up within whichever level holds focus (from the trigger, ArrowDown enters the list), ArrowRight opens a submenu **and** enters it, ArrowLeft closes it and hands focus back to the parent row. Escape stays on the existing document listener, now leaving the submenu first and closing the menu only when none is open. The outside-pointerdown rule counts the portaled card as inside (without it the card's own click never landed). Extracted `clampedToViewport` / `rowsOf` helpers, shared with the existing list placement. Parent row: `role="menuitem"` + `aria-haspopup="menu"` + `aria-expanded`, never selects, trailing `IconChevronRightOutline14` marker, `data-menu-submenu` for the keyboard.
- `src/Menu.module.css` — `.submenu` is `position: fixed`, z 1101 (above the portaled list, itself above modal overlays); the pointer bridge `::before` kept, scrolling moved to an inner `.viewport` so the bridge is not clipped; `.scrollable` conditional class deleted — every card now caps at `calc(100vh - 24px)` and scrolls its items, which the old submenu implementation had to opt out of; `.itemWrap` is now the submenu row's hover region only; new `.more`.
- `src/index.ts` — exports `MenuSubmenu`.
- `tests/atoms.client.spec.tsx` — rewrote the submenu test and added four: hover open + nested selection + close on leave/crossing a plain row; portaled card counted as inside for outside-click; ArrowRight/Down/Up/Left traversal and focus return; Escape leaving the submenu before the menu; a `@ts-expect-error` proof that a submenu cannot hold a submenu. Deleted the obsolete "caps the list height unless a submenu row is present" test (the class it asserted no longer exists).
- `README.md` / `README.zh.md` / `README.i18n.yaml` — new "## Menus" / "## 菜单" section.

**`packages/client/ui-shell`**
- `src/client/Taskbar.tsx` — `LAUNCH_PREFIX = 'launch:'`; a Surface with ≥1 launches renders as a submenu whose first item is the plain open (`t('taskbar.launcher.open', { label })`, id = the surface id) followed by one row per launch (`launch:&lt;surfaceId&gt;:&lt;launchId&gt;`); zero-launch Surfaces stay flat. New `onOpenLaunch` prop; `onSelect` routes the launch prefix by splitting at the first colon past it. Switch-space and New-space groups untouched. Module doc updated.
- `src/client/SpaceFrame.tsx`, `src/client/ShellRoot.tsx` — `onOpenLaunch` threaded through `SpaceFrameInputs` to `ctx.spaces.openLaunch`.
- `src/client/locales.ts` — new `taskbar.launcher.open`: zh `打开{label}` / en `Open {label}` (interpolating the Surface label reads better than a bare "Open" and gives each row a self-describing accessible name).
- `tests/frame-bench.client.tsx` — new `launching(entry, ...launches)` helper; `onOpenLaunch` added to the recorded acts.
- `tests/shell-chrome.client.spec.tsx` — two launcher tests: submenu structure (flat neighbour keeps no `aria-haspopup`, card lists `Open Chat` + both launches) with the default open routing to `onOpenSurface`; picking a launch calls `onOpenLaunch('chat', 'plugins')`.
- `tests/shell-root.client.spec.tsx` — pins the wiring: the launch row reaches `ctx.spaces.openLaunch` with both ids and no launch state.
- `README.md` / `README.zh.md` / `README.i18n.yaml` — launcher paragraph describes the submenu.

## Commands and results
- `npx tsc -b tsconfig.client.json` — clean (exit 0)
- `npx vitest run packages/client/{ui-primitives,ui-shell,space-runtime}/tests` — 32 files, **681 passed**
- `npx vitest run` over every other Menu consumer (locale, ui-agent-preset, ui-conversation, ui-note, ui-permission-presets, ui-workspace) — 58 files, **902 passed**
- `npx tsx scripts/run-oxlint.ts packages/client/ui-primitives packages/client/ui-shell` — exit 0 (no directives touched)
- `pnpm run build` — exit 0, 214 client artifacts recorded (run last, after every edit)
- `pnpm run verify-translation-pairing --write &lt;both en READMEs&gt;` then check — both pairs clean; the only failure left is `packages/bundle/agent-standard/README.*`, a parallel lane's file
- `pnpm run verify-export-jsdoc`, `pnpm run knip` — clean
- `pnpm run duplication` — fails on one **pre-existing** clone in `packages/client/ui-renderer/src/client/scoped-slots.tsx` (unmodified file, committed in phase 3); no clones from my files
- `pnpm run doc-sync` — 5 gates fail, all owned by parallel lanes: surface-chat README model-experience, `agentPresets.resolveFor`/`PresetRequest` type-link (doc graphs + cordis catalog), and slot-catalog line drift from `ui-chat-surface`. I regenerated `slot-catalog.ts` to confirm the drift was purely ui-chat-surface line numbers, then reverted it so it stays that lane's to record. `verify-config-catalog` passed on re-run (another lane wrote `docs/config-catalog.md` mid-run).

## Deviations
1. **Removed** `MenuItem.submenu` rather than leaving two submenu mechanisms; it had no product consumer. `type?: never` on `MenuItem` is what makes "no nested submenu" a compile error — without a discriminant, a submenu object satisfies `MenuItem` structurally.
2. The portalled card let the `.scrollable` opt-out die, so long launcher lists now scroll instead of overflowing the viewport (the old code deliberately gave submenu-bearing menus no height cap).

## BLOCKER — production regression, stopped as instructed

`space-switcher.e2e.ts` and `space-shared-preset.e2e.ts` fail, and **not** on the submenu-parent reality. The launcher's Surface order changed: expected `Chat, Notes, Plugin Studio` → observed `Notes, Plugin Studio, Chat`, reproduced identically across four independent Host boots.

Root cause, in `packages/space/surface-chat/src/index.ts`: `apply` was `export function apply(ctx): void` at HEAD~ and is now `export async function apply(ctx): Promise&lt;void&gt;`, awaiting `declaredLaunches(ctx)` (a real fs scan of the preset roots) **before** `ctx.effect(() =&gt; ctx.spaces.registerSurface(...))`. `SurfaceCatalog` is documented as "in registration order", and `surface-note` / `surface-plugin-studio` both still register synchronously — so Chat now lands last even though `app-chat` loads before `app-notes`. This reorders both the launcher menu and the model-facing `space_list` catalog, and the ordering is a race between the roster read and the remaining bundle loads, so it is timing-dependent rather than pinned.

I left both assertions unchanged: rewriting them to `Notes, Plugin Studio, Chat` would bake in an unnoticed product-visible reordering of the flagship application, which fails "every accepted diff must be explainable". Nothing in the working tree (including the refreshed `space-tools` golden, whose reorder is fixture-driven) shows this consequence being seen or accepted. Fix belongs in the D3 lane — e.g. register the Surface synchronously and resolve launches without an await in front of the registration.

- The hero mode chip (`packages/client/ui-agent-preset`) is **one controller and one store per client, not per Chat Window**: `seat.store` is shared by every registration and `compose(sessionId)` keeps a single field, so with two Chat Windows open both chips show whichever Session was composed last. My spec therefore asserts the untouched Window's mode off the durable record and keeps the chip assertion only for the just-launched Window (documented in the spec).

<result>## 1. Overall architecture (docs/architecture.md)

**What the harness is.** DeepSeek Harness (`dsh`) is a plugin tree built on **Cordis**, a vendored plugin framework (`docs/cordis-primer.md`; source and sync procedure in `vendor/README.md`). Plugins contribute *services*, *typed events*, and *reversible effects* to a shared context.

**"Everything is a plugin," concretely:** the model adapter, the tool registry, the session log, and the agent loop itself are all plugins, so each is replaceable from configuration. The doc's exact claim: "There is no privileged core to patch: you extend dsh by mounting a plugin beside the others, and registrations are effects that unwind when their plugin unloads."

**Product API spine** — `packages/core/` ("Product API spine: sessions, prompts, tools, agent services, and the concrete loop"), documented in architecture.md as a table of package → owns → `ctx` key:

| Package | Owns | ctx key |
|---|---|---|
| `core/session` | append-only `SessionEvent` log + in-memory store | `ctx.sessions` |
| `core/system-prompt` | prompt-section and tool-schema assembly | `ctx.systemPrompt` |
| `core/tools` | scoped tool registry + guarded execution pipeline | `ctx.tools` |
| `core/agent` | the `Agent` interface, live registry, `agent/*` events | `ctx.agents` |
| `core/agent-loop` | the default driver implementing that interface | `ctx.agentLoop` |
| `core/scope` | per-agent scoped-registration primitive | library, no key |
| `llm/llm` | message/stream vocabulary + adapter seam | `ctx.llm` |

**Turn flow.** A **step** = one model request plus the tools it calls; a **turn** = zero or more steps, opening before its first input is claimed and closing "once nothing is owed." The documented sequence: `turn/start` → claim next-step input plus one queued message → assemble prompt sections + tool schemas → `agent/pre-step` (reject | enter(messages)) → `step/start` → append entered messages as `user/message` → derive model history from the log → `agent/request` → `llm/stream` → `assistant/chunk*` → `assistant/message` → `tool/call*` → `tools/pre-execute` → `tools/execute` → `tools/post-execute` → `tool/result*` → `step/end` → (loop if tools owe another request or next-step input arrived) → `agent/turn-stopping` → `turn/end`.

Durable session events: `turn/*`, `step/*`, `user/message`, `assistant/*`, `tool/*`. Live extension points: everything else, across three domains. **Waterfalls** (listeners must call `next()` to delegate): `agent/pre-step`, `agent/request`, `llm/stream`, and the three `tools/*` events. `agent/turn-stopping` is **serial** with no `next()`. Input reaches the driver through **one inbox**; some messages wake it immediately, injected context waits until another message does. A rejected or empty first claim still closes a durable turn that spent no step, "so the log records the attempt."

**Three event domains:** *session events* (durable facts appended to the log, broadcast through `session/event`), *agent events* (`agent/*`, carrying a live `Agent`: inbox, step, status, request, validation, continuation), *capability events* (`fs/*`, `tools/*`, `telemetry/*` — attach policy/adapters to a seam without importing the loop). Full producer/consumer index: `docs/event-producer-consumer.md`.

**Session log / "model-visible ⟺ logged".** The log is the source of the context the model sees; `deriveMessages()` projects model history from it, and raw `assistant/chunk` events preserve replay and UI fidelity. Fork, resume, transcripts, telemetry, and persistence all derive from this stream. The invariant, verbatim: "**Model-visible means logged.** Anything that reaches a model request must be reconstructable from the log, and a runtime invariant asserts it." Consequence: a new model-visible input requires a new session event — extend `SessionEventMap` and render from the log.

## 2. Capability seams

A **seam** is a swappable capability with three roles: a **Service Definition** (interface), a **Service Provider** (implementation), and a **Consumer** (user, commonly a model-facing tool). "A package may combine roles, but one role alone is not a seam; adding a capability means designing all three."

The glossary is stricter: the Service Definition is the Cordis `Service` that owns its `ctx.&lt;key&gt;` and vocabulary types — an abstract class (e.g. `ShellExecutor`) or a concrete registry (e.g. `WebRuntime`), **never a TypeScript `interface`**. Canonical example, `packages/shell`: **`dsh-shell`** (Service Definition), **`dsh-bash-local`** / **`dsh-bash-sandbox`** (providers), **`dsh-tool-bash`** (Consumer). Roles normally live in separate packages when they evolve independently; `dsh-llm` legitimately owns both its Service Definition and Consumer. "The seam is the complete capability, never one role."

Why it matters: one provider swap changes the whole product — filesystem and subprocess providers share one execution world, so pointing them at a remote sandbox moves Bash, PTY, and LSP with them, with no provider forks. Subagent providers "vary just as widely behind one interface, from a fresh child agent to a delegated turn in another product." **Experimental Agent Teams** is a private opt-in coordination seam on `ctx.agentTeams` (durable roster, task board, mailbox over continuable subagents).

Related glossary concepts: **agent scope** (contributions are global or scoped to exactly one scope key — a live agent is its own scope key; two levels, flat, no inheritance to subagents), **shadowing** (most-specific-wins: a scoped tool/section/variable replaces its same-named global twin for that scope alone), **restriction** (`tools.restrict` filters the global tool set for one scope, composed by intersection; a filtered-away tool is absent from the prompt *and* refuses execution), **setup window** (`CreateAgentOptions.setup` — registers, never drives), **lineage** (`parentSession`, durable `delegationDepth`, runtime `subagentDepth`; data only, never affects visibility). Loop hierarchy: **turn** ⊃ **step**; **round** is an outer policy iteration (goal round, Ralph attempt).

`shell/` Bash capability family: executor seam, local impl, model-facing tool · `terminal/` Persistent PTY capability family: owner-scoped sessions, local impl, model-facing tools · `code-runtime/` route server · `client/` Web-GUI browser half: shell, wire, object services, slots, `ui-*` plugins.

**POC:** `e2b/` E2B providers. **Unreleased:** `experimental/` Private prototypes and internal-only plugins. **Support:** `examples/` Demo bundles (agent-spine + CLI/ACP/JSON-RPC bins) leaves load — "Support — example infra"; `test-support/` Support infrastructure (testkits, invariants, replay, Loader smokes) — lower compatibility expectations; `util/` Zero-dependency utilities shared across groups (`Branded&lt;B&gt;`, Harness home/path helpers, timeout, retention) — small, stable, harness-dep-free.

**Layering rules stated:** "New packages join existing groups; new groups update their README and this table." The dependency graph is generated at `docs/module-graph.md` (`pnpm run gen-module-graph`, freshness-gated in CI). The core rule, verbatim: "**Extension plugins depend on Service Definitions, never concrete providers.** `dsh-agent-loop` is swappable; UI, hook, and tool plugins use `dsh-agent`. Composition bundles, including `dsh-agent-spine-demo`, may depend on spine plugins."

## 4. Composition: profiles, bundles, presets, patches

A running `dsh` is a plugin tree composed at boot from ordered layers.

- **Profile** — a named composition stored in the Harness home. It lists the bundles it stacks, holds any out-of-tree plugins it installs, and keeps the user's own `cordis.patch.yml`. `web` and `headless` ship as templates.
- **Bundle** — a distribution format for Cordis config rows and the code they mount, "so whatever it inserts stays patchable by the layers above it." Manifest-declared: `"dsh": { "bundle": { "patch": "./cordis.patch.yml" } }`; `dsh.profile` lists a profile's bundles.
- **Host layers every profile starts from:** `dsh-runtime-core` (model adapters, session persistence, telemetry), `dsh-agent-standard` (standard agent's tools, delegation, compaction, approval policy), `dsh-local-machine` (executors, sandbox, settings, credentials, durable roots). Over them: `dsh-web-platform` (browser delivery: server, transport, client runtime + runtime glue plugin), composed by `dsh-space-shell`, `dsh-app-chat`, `dsh-app-settings`, `dsh-app-notes`, `dsh-personal-plugin-runtime`; or `dsh-headless`, a one-shot runner with no server at all. `web` takes all nine bundles; `headless` takes the three host layers plus `headless/`.
- **Layer order** applied to an empty entry list: each bundle in the profile's listed order → the profile's `cordis.patch.yml` → the home-level one → any `--patch` overlay. A patch targets a row by id and replaces its whole config, or inserts new rows. Inspect the real tree with `dsh --profile web --dump-config`; any printed row can be replaced by a patch. Mechanics in `packages/boot/app-boot/README.md#profiles`; fields in the generated `docs/config-catalog.md`.
- **Agent preset** (`packages/preset/`) — a directory holding one `agent.cordis.yml`; mounting it under an agent's *scope context* gives that session its own tools and prompt sections while other live sessions keep theirs, so one process runs several differently composed agents at once. `ctx.agentPresets` owns vocabulary, filesystem discovery over trusted and user-authored roots, and the guarded per-agent mount; `persona/` makes agent identity a composable row. Shipped presets live in `apps/cli/config/agent-presets/`. Split rule: registries and cross-session facilities are process singletons that stay in the host composition; a preset carries only what one agent contributes to them, and a preset naming a row that publishes a process-global service is **rejected at mount**. Architecture.md's matching line: "Give one session a different capability set → compose an agent preset; a service row there needs an `isolate` realm."
- Out-of-tree bundles install via `dsh plugin --profile &lt;name&gt; add &lt;package&gt;`.

**Cordis mechanics that composition leans on** (`docs/cordis-primer.md`): a plugin is an object implementing `Service` (function with optional `inject`/`apply(ctx)`, or a `Service` subclass); a context is a repository of services keyed by `ctx.&lt;key&gt;`; `inject` declares service dependency so load order is expressed as requirements rather than manual boot sequencing; typed events via TypeScript declaration merging; registrations are reversible effects installed through `ctx.effect()`/`ctx.on()`. Dispatch modes are part of an event's public contract, tagged `@mode` so the generated catalog can check declarations against dispatch sites: `emit` (not awaited, registration order, no return), `waterfall` (not awaited, registration order, returns), `parallel` (awaited, listeners in parallel, no return), `serial` (awaited, registration order, returns). Waterfall is **around-middleware**: a listener receives `(...args, next)`; call `next()` to delegate, return without it to short-circuit (the design for single-decision events); `prepend: true` only when a listener must run before ordinary registrations. Loader config: `@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-include` parses `!!js` into expression nodes; Loader interpolates an entry's `config` after declared injections activate (against that plugin context) and `disabled` at every mount decision (against the loader context); other entry metadata stays literal.

## 5. Client/web side and how it meets the server

From architecture.md's "where new behavior goes" table: add UI or editor integration by driving `ctx.agents` and rendering from `session/event`; add a launchable **Surface** by registering a `SurfaceDefinition` on `ctx.spaces`, whose Client renderer keys into the `space.window.surface` slot; add a Web Client Chat node by registering a `ConversationNodeDefinition` plus a keyed renderer; install a durable personal plugin by promoting a draft package through `ctx.personalPlugins` — a person approves it in the **Plugin Studio** and its Surfaces come from the package manifest.

The GUI is split into two halves. **`host/`** (web-GUI host half) is the API gateway every client shape shares plus the plain HTTP server it rides on: `apiproxy` (`ctx.apiProxy`, transport-independent), `webserver` (`ctx.webServer`), `frontend-static` (SPA dist on the webserver fallback seat), the `directory-picker` seam with `-native`/`-browse`/`-auto` backends, and `plugin-inventory` (read-only projection of Loader entries, Remote `pluginInventory/list`). The composed application is `apps/cli` booting the `dsh-web-platform` bundle serving `apps/web`.

**`client/`** is the browser half — all packages named `@deepseek-ai/dsh-client-&lt;name&gt;` (except `test-runtime`): `web` (boots the browser shell from the client entry graph), `ui-renderer`, `modules`, `connection` (browser–host RPC and event delivery), `runtime` (shared slot registry, snapshot-store engine, single Host connection stream), `session-runtime` (browser Session object layer + conversation assembly registries), `workspace-runtime`, `space-runtime` (mirrors the Host's durable Space record and Surface catalog into the browser), `hmr`, `locale`, `ui-slots`, `ui-theme`, `ui-primitives`, plus feature plugins `ui-shell` (shell frame, draws each open Window), `ui-chat-surface`, `ui-chat-sidebar`, `ui-note`, `ui-conversation`, `ui-tool`, `ui-workflow-run`, `ui-goal`, `ui-trajectory`, `ui-commands`, `ui-input-trigger`, `ui-skill`, `ui-reference`, `ui-subagent`, `ui-jobs`, `ui-attachment`, `ui-workspace`, `ui-brand-official`.

**Wire path.** `api/remotes` owns Host Agent/Session lookup policy and Client Remote contribution assembly (no service; configures `ctx.typert`, consumes `ctx.remote`); `api/gateway` is the Host Typert dispatcher and Client Remote endpoint (`ctx.typertGateway` / `ctx.remote`). Runtime dependency direction is **`remotes → gateway → connection → webserver`**: the BFF consumes the shared `TypertClientRemote` contract, Gateway delegates transport to Connection, Connection mounts on the HTTP server; Cordis service injection and Client module metadata preserve this order without importing the concrete Gateway from the Remotes Client entry. Legacy `host/apiproxy` remains the fallback for methods not yet migrated to Remote, consuming the `api-remotes` Host resolver so migrated and legacy methods share one Agent/Session identity policy.

**Typert** separates source analysis, runtime storage, and Loader discovery: `registry/` stores runtime package reflection and schemas (`ctx.typert`), `loader/` discovers Loader entries and registers generated host artifacts (consumes `ctx.loader` and `ctx.typert`), `generator/` is a build-time library generating runtime artifacts from source types.

**SDK** is the out-of-process story, distinct from the browser GUI: `sdk/protocol` (SDK runtime wire protocol), `sdk/client` (TypeScript client API driving a Harness runtime), `sdk/server` (serves out-of-process SDK clients over stdio JSON-RPC). Callers supply the runtime executable and its `cordis.yml`; the group explicitly does not create, configure, build, or launch developer projects. `acp/` provides an automation-only Agent Client Protocol server.

- **`apps/cli`** = `@deepseek-ai/dsh`, bin `dsh` → `lib/bin.js`. The product launcher for **profiles**: ordered stacks of bundle patch layers under user overrides. Entry modes: `dsh --profile &lt;name&gt;`, `dsh --profile headless "job"`, `dsh web` (alias of `--profile web`), `dsh plugin --profile &lt;name&gt; &lt;pnpm args&gt;`. `src/args.ts` owns the grammar; `src/bin.ts` loads only the selected runner. Composition order: empty root → each `dsh.profile.bundles` patch → profile `cordis.patch.yml` → `$DSH_HOME/cordis.patch.yml` → `--patch` overlays. Assembles all 10 bundles plus ~50 tool/feature packages directly. Also has `composition.md`, `config/`, `reference/`.
- **`apps/web`** = `@deepseek-ai/dsh-web-frontend`. Thin vite build over the `@deepseek-ai/dsh-client-web` shell library; **zero workspace `dependencies`** (all devDeps). Ships `dist/`, served by `apps/cli`'s `dsh web`.

## Non-npm trees

- **`examples/`** (repo root, one workspace member + module-resolution root): runnable `cordis.yml` **leaves** — `headless-agent`, `jsonrpc-agent`, `acp-agent`, `web-cordis`, `web-schedule`, `mcp-memory`. Per `/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/examples/AGENTS.md`: not a build target; `examples/package.json` declares the union of every leaf's plugins so a plain-node boot resolves through real `exports`. Each leaf carries a **keyless** Loader smoke and a **with-key** live-model e2e (self-skips without `DEEPSEEK_API_KEY`). Reusable logic must be extracted to `packages/`.
- **`python/`**: `sdk` (`deepseek-harness-sdk`, high-level turns API + JSON-RPC client) and `sdk-runtime` (`deepseek-harness-runtime-bin`, bundled runtime binaries; also the single-exe deploy root).
- **`native/`**: `landlock-run` — Landlock self-restrict-then-exec launcher, three-package npm family with per-platform optional deps.
- **`website/`**: VitePress config + `docs.ts` publication manifest only; canonical prose stays in `docs/`, projected into ignored `.generated/`.
- **`vendor/`**: pinned Cordis framework sources — `cordis`, `cosmokit`, `schemastery`, `loader`, `group`, `include`, `hmr`, `timer`, `logger-console`.

## Layering (bottom → top)

1. **`invariants`** (universal, 255 dependents) → **`util`** (brand/timeout/home-paths).
2. **`typert`**, **`storage`**, **`settings`**, **`identity`**, **`subprocess`** — infra with no harness-concept deps.
3. **`llm`** and **`core`** (session/agent/tools/system-prompt) — the spine every feature imports. `core` + `interaction` are mutually referencing at group granularity (different packages, not a package cycle).
4. **Capability seams**: fs, shell, sandbox, terminal, web, skill, jobs, spill, attachment, credentials, code-runtime, compaction, subagent — each depends on core + llm + invariants + util and nothing above.
5. **Domain features**: goal, note, space, workflow, automation, session-query, plugin-runtime, workspace, schedule, plan, todo, hooks, guard, preset.
6. **Transports &amp; GUI**: sdk, acp → **api** → **host** / **client** (api ↔ client is bidirectional at group level).
7. **`bundle`** — patch layers assembling everything (41 groups).
8. **`apps/cli`** on top; **`apps/web`** builds the client shell into `dist/`.

Cross-cutting: `dsh-session` (94), `dsh-llm` (93), `dsh-agent` (70), `dsh-tools` (54), `dsh-client-runtime` (45), `dsh-brand` (37). Stated rule in `packages/README.md`: **extension plugins depend on Service Definitions, never concrete providers**; `dsh-agent-loop` is swappable, so UI/hook/tool plugins depend on `dsh-agent`, not the loop. Generated graph: `/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/docs/module-graph.md` (`pnpm run gen-module-graph`, CI freshness-gated).</result>

## Vendored Cordis in deepseek-harness

### What Cordis is

Cordis is an upstream, MIT-licensed (`Copyright (c) 2021-present Shigma`) TypeScript plugin framework — per its own README, for "applications that need explicit dependency injection, scoped services, lifecycle-managed cleanup, and optional configuration-driven loading." Upstream lives at `https://github.com/cordiverse/cordis`.

Per `/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/docs/cordis-primer.md`, the upstream-standard model is: a plugin is an object implementing `Service` (function with `inject`/`apply(ctx)`, or a `Service` subclass); a context is a repository of services claimed at stable `ctx.&lt;key&gt;`; dependencies are declared via `inject` so load order comes from service requirements, not manual boot sequencing; typed events are merged in via TS declaration merging and dispatched as `emit` / `waterfall` / `parallel` / `serial`; registrations are reversible effects through `ctx.effect()` / `ctx.on()`. `ctx.waterfall` is around-middleware: a listener gets `(...args, next)`, calls `next()` to delegate or returns without it to short-circuit. All of that is upstream Cordis, not a harness addition. The harness conventions layered on top are documentation/tooling rules — new harness events carry an `@mode` tag so the generated catalog can check declarations against dispatch sites.

### Vendored packages (manifest, `/Users/ren/repos/deepseek-harness/vendor/README.md`)

Nine packages, source-vendored (not npm deps) so the harness "fully owns its framework layer (auditable, patchable, pinned)." Upstream workspace `cordis-workspace` (local checkout `~/repos/cordis-workspace`).

| Dir | npm name | Upstream name | Version | Repo | Commit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `cosmokit/` | `@deepseek-ai/cosmokit` | `cosmokit` | 1.8.1 | deepseek-harness/cosmokit | `16f6fc058ade66e8ac5da0033d35a8d0f279f544` |
| `schemastery/` | `@deepseek-ai/schemastery` | `schemastery` | 3.18.0 | deepseek-harness/schemastery (`packages/core`) | `e67cee00ad725bd1534aee930a979ea3eec6f698` |
| `cordis/` | `@deepseek-ai/cordis` | `cordis` | 4.0.0-rc.7 | cordiverse/cordis (`packages/core`) | `56b3d4f725681cf4556c1a8695a709cc3b6eed74` |
| `loader/` | `@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-loader` | `@cordisjs/plugin-loader` | 1.0.0-rc.5 | cordiverse/cordis (`packages/loader`) | `56b3d4f725681cf4556c1a8695a709cc3b6eed74` |
| `include/` | `@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-include` | `@cordisjs/plugin-include` | 1.0.4 | deepseek-harness/cordis | `abb0a307cb1d3b0947f455d590cf5ba922d4caa4` |
| `group/` | `@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-group` | `@cordisjs/plugin-group` | 1.0.0 | deepseek-harness/cordis | `abb0a307cb1d3b0947f455d590cf5ba922d4caa4` |
| `timer/` | `@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-timer` | `@cordisjs/plugin-timer` | 1.1.2 | deepseek-harness/cordis | `abb0a307cb1d3b0947f455d590cf5ba922d4caa4` |
| `hmr/` | `@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-hmr` | `@cordisjs/plugin-hmr` | 1.0.15 | deepseek-harness/cordis | `abb0a307cb1d3b0947f455d590cf5ba922d4caa4` |
| `logger-console/` | `@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-logger-console` | `@cordisjs/plugin-logger-console` | 1.0.0 | deepseek-harness/cordis | `abb0a307cb1d3b0947f455d590cf5ba922d4caa4` |

Note two of the three SHAs point at `deepseek-harness/*` forks, not `cordiverse/cordis`; only `cordis/` and `loader/` come straight from cordiverse. `ls vendor` matches exactly these nine plus `README.md` and `AGENTS.md` (with `CLAUDE.md` a symlink to it). Each package has `src/`, `lib/`, `package.json`, `tsconfig.json`, `LICENSE`, `README.md`; `cordis/` adds `bin.js`; `schemastery/`, `loader/`, `logger-console/` add `tsdown.config.ts`. `vendor/cordis/src/` is `context.ts, events.ts, fiber.ts, index.ts, logger.ts, reflect.ts, registry.ts, service.ts, utils.ts`.

Third-party deps stay on npm: `@standard-schema/spec`, `js-yaml`, `chokidar`, `picomatch`, `@babel/code-frame`, `supports-color`, `node-addon-require-builtin`. Deliberately **not** vendored (verified unused): `reggol`, `@cordisjs/utils`, `@cordisjs/element`, `@cordisjs/unyaml`.

### Rescoping and privacy

`docs/rescope.md` is the name mapping. Rule: `cordis` → `@deepseek-ai/cordis`, `@cordisjs/plugin-&lt;x&gt;` → `@deepseek-ai/cordis-plugin-&lt;x&gt;`. **Why:** every harness package declares the framework as a *peer* dependency, so publishing the harness publishes this layer with it — publishing under the upstream names "would squat them on the registry." Manifests carry `private: true` for the same reason (mod #2).

Unchanged by the rename: directory names, version numbers, and dependency *ranges* (only the key changes — `"cordis": "^4.0.0-rc.7"` → `"@deepseek-ai/cordis": "^4.0.0-rc.7"`), so the tree still reads as an upstream snapshot. Also untouched: the Loader's `cordis:` builtin protocol prefix (`cordis:include`, `cordis:group`); the `cordis.yml` config family (`*.cordis.yml`, `*.cordis.snapshot.yml`, `cordis.patch.yml`); harness packages like `@deepseek-ai/dsh-tool-cordis`; upstream runtime identifiers (`Symbol.for('schemastery')`, Schemastery's `vendor:` metadata field); and prose outside `docs/`. Subpath exports keep their path.

Resolution is via `pnpm-workspace.yaml#linkWorkspacePackages`, which makes preserved semver ranges resolve to the pinned workspaces. Two gates enforce it: `verify-vendored-links` (hygiene) asserts every vendored name resolves to a workspace `link:` in `pnpm-lock.yaml` with no registry copy alongside, and `pnpm run rescope-vendor:check`. `scripts/rescope-vendor.ts` owns the mapping; `--apply`, `--apply --reverse`, and a bare report mode exist. Schemastery additionally gets a conditional `exports` map (import → `.mjs`, require → `.cjs`) because pnpm links the directory and Node's ESM resolver would otherwise fall back to `main` and load the CJS entry, whose lazy `require('@deepseek-ai/cosmokit')` can race ESM loading of the same linked module under vitest.

### Complete local-modification log (18 entries)

The README states the rule: "Keep this log exhaustive — every divergence from upstream must be listed." `vendor/AGENTS.md` reinforces: do not edit `vendor/*/src/` casually; `tsconfig.json` is the one exception.

1. **`hmr/src/index.ts`** — removed `./locales/en-US.yml` / `zh-CN.yml` imports, the `.i18n({...})` call on `Config`, and `src/locales/`. Those imports need the `@cordisjs/unyaml` runtime YAML hook, which isn't vendored; the texts only localize config descriptions.
2. **All `package.json`** — regenerated: `private: true`; precise `files` for bundled runtime plus `lib/types/**/*.d.ts`/`.d.ts.map`; `src` kept in `files` only where the previous list shipped it; added `./src/*` export where missing; declaration metadata pointed at `lib/types`; upstream `devDependencies`/`scripts`/`repository` removed. Ranges preserved except `hmr` declaring `esbuild` as a direct dev dep (its source imports the `BuildFailure` type; pnpm strict resolution requires the owner package to name it) and `loader` requiring `node-addon-require-builtin@^0.1.4` to match published app packages' runtime.
3. **All `tsconfig.json`** — regenerated to extend repo-root `tsconfig.base.json`, emit to `lib/types`, declare project references.
4. **Internal specifiers** — local relative imports/exports changed to explicit `.ts` specifiers so TS rewrites emitted JS to `.js` while declarations stay NodeNext-safe; includes `loader/src/config/isolate.ts` using `declare module './entry.ts'`.
5. **`schemastery/tsdown.config.ts`, `logger-console/tsdown.config.ts`** — ours, not upstream: per-package build-shape overrides (dual ESM+CJS; separate node/browser entries) reading JS emitted under `lib/types` and writing publish entries under `lib/`. Not part of the upstream sync surface.
6. **`cordis/src/fiber.ts` lifecycle hardening** — closes three reentrant disposal gaps. Effect owner-list wrapper registered before setup runs, so unload begun inside setup awaits setup and all collected cleanup; sync setup failure removes the wrapper and rolls back. Async cleanup stays owner-visible until quiescence; internal effect composition joins a running cleanup while repeated public disposer calls keep upstream single-shot results. Effect creation rejected while owner is `UNLOADING` (`PENDING`/`LOADING` stay legal). Child fibers get their parent-owned disposer before `internal/plugin` publication, resolve dependency declarations added by that notification before activation, drain effects attached while pending, skip plugin execution when reentrant disposal invalidates the load epoch before its first checkpoint, and contain teardown-notification failures per observer. `Fiber.update()` returns its `internal/update` waterfall result so Loader callers can await a restart while keeping synchronous config validation.
7. **`cordis/src/*.ts` JSDoc enrichment** — `@param`/`@returns` and contract docs (disposal semantics, waterfall veto, bail conditions, error cases) across `Context` (class, statics, interface props incl. `root`), `EventsService`, `Fiber`, `RegistryService`, `ReflectService`, `Service`, `LoggerService` and their `declare module './context.ts'` overloads. Comment-only. Motivation: the website API-reference generator hard-errors on undocumented members. Explicitly marked to retire once upstreamed to the fork.
8. **Transactional Loader/Include config reconciliation** — Loader imports a changed entry name before disposal, awaits lifecycle settlement, restores the previous plugin/config on failure; settlement rechecks service-gated fibers after tasks drain, rejects failures, leaves absent-dependency fibers pending. Group starts candidates concurrently, awaits every outcome, contains sibling-start failures after their tree is disposed, undoes changes/additions on live-update failure, awaits removal, preserves programmatic option identity, persists only after success. Include validates detached candidate content, patches a clone, reconciles, then commits cached content/data. Non-array parse invalid; patches re-apply on every file or Include-config update; omitted patch list clears the overlay; initial content falls back to `initial` only on `ENOENT`. Covered by `packages/boot/app-boot/tests/config-reload.spec.ts`, `packages/host/webserver/tests/webserver.spec.ts`.
9. **`hmr/src/index.ts` exact config watching** — `registerConfig()` watches one absolute path outside module roots (including under missing parents), serializes and coalesces refreshes, returns an async disposer. Module watches realpath the existing base dir, attach listeners before declaring ready, use that spelling for Node module-cache identity; exact-config watches realpath the deepest existing ancestor and restore the missing suffix — preventing Windows short-name aliases colliding with long-form libuv paths. Refresh failures normalized to `Error`, logged, broadcast via parallel `hmr/config-update-failed`; observer failures contained. Covered by `packages/boot/app-boot/tests/hmr-config.spec.ts`.
10. **Node-compatible TypeScript** — erased imports marked explicitly across `cordis`, `loader`, `include`, `hmr`, `schemastery` so Node's native TS transform doesn't request types as runtime exports. Schemastery uses ESM default export with `type: module`; built entries retain explicit `.mjs`/`.cjs`.
11. **`include/src/index.ts` patch-semantics export** — private `applyPatches` body extracted into exported pure `applyEntryPatches(data, patches, warn)`; `!!js` YAML dialect exported as `entryListSchema`, so `dsh --dump-config` prints exactly what the include would mount without booting a tree. Behavior-preserving for mounting; exists so config tooling never reimplements (and drifts from) the patch algorithm. **Also a real bug fix:** `applyEntryPatches` indexes each `insert`ed entry as it is added, so a later patch in the same list can configure or disable a row an earlier patch inserted — *upstream built the id index once before the patch loop, leaving inserted rows silently unpatchable.* Matters because `dsh` composes empty profile root + bundle patch layer + profile and home `cordis.patch.yml` + `--patch` overlays as sibling patch lists at one include level, and patches never cross an include boundary.
12. **`include/src/index.ts` serialized child-tree mutation + `hmr/src/index.ts` initial-scan suppression** — every Include child-tree mutation (initial apply, refresh, `internal/update` re-application) runs through one per-Include queue, because the group's transactional `update` is not reentrant: two concurrent applies interleave create and rollback and strand the Include fiber. HMR main watcher now passes `ignoreInitial: true` — the initial scan re-announced files boot had consumed, and its `add` for a config file refreshed an Include mid-initial-apply; once serialized this deadlocked (rollback disposed HMR, whose teardown drain waited on a refresh queued behind that same apply) and exited 13 with no diagnostic. `registerConfig()` keeps `ignoreInitial: false` because a user patch layer present at registration must apply once. Covered by the patch-overlay boot-failure case in `apps/cli/tests/built-bin.e2e.ts`.
13. **`include/src/index.ts` `writeTask` type** — widened `writeTask?: NodeJS.Timeout` to `NodeJS.Timeout | undefined`; the debounced writer assigns `undefined` on flush, which `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` rejects on a plain optional. Type-only.
14. **`include/src/index.ts` durable debounced writes** — serialized/tracked config writes, bounded-backoff retry of transient `EACCES`/`EBUSY`/`EPERM` rename failures, observed async timer rejections, drained latest write on Include teardown. Windows can briefly retain a destination handle after a Loader child disposes; upstream's fire-and-forget rename escaped as an unhandled rejection and could lose persisted `disabled` state. Terminal failure stays on the queue so `Include.stop()` rethrows rather than silently declaring persistence complete. Covered by `packages/host/directory-picker-auto/tests/loader-composition.spec.ts`.
15. **Lazy Loader config resolution** across `cordis/src/{events,fiber}.ts`, `loader/src/{index,config/entry}.ts`, `include/src/index.ts`, `hmr/src/index.ts` — ports upstream PR [cordiverse/cordis#41](https://github.com/cordiverse/cordis/pull/41): retains raw fiber config, resolves through `internal/config` only after declared injections are active. Provider replacement re-resolves; pending updates retain raw; HMR transfers it. Applies only to the entry root, so child plugins keep caller-owned config identity. Include declares the `EntryGroup.key` tree-carrier marker (as Group does) so interpolation keeps entry/patch lists literal and a nested row's `!!js` resolves lazily in that row's own fiber. Deferred failures retain the owning row diagnostic; tree teardown doesn't persist failure-driven self-disposal.
16. **`cordis/package.json` publishes `src`** — added `src` to `files`, joining the other eight. Cordis declares `"./src/*": "./src/*"`, so a tarball without `src` publishes an export map pointing at absent files; the release change judgement also reads `files` to decide whether a diff reaches the payload.
17. **`@deepseek-ai` rescope** — every manifest `name`, internal dependency entry, and module specifier uses the scoped names. Directory names, versions, ranges unchanged; no upstream runtime identifier renamed. Re-apply with `pnpm run rescope-vendor --apply` after a sync.
18. **Entry `disabled` interpolation in `loader/src/config/entry.ts`** — a `disabled: !!js` expression evaluates against the loader context at every mount decision; the raw node stays in options so write-back keeps the `!!js` form. `disabled` is the only interpolated metadata field. Covered by `packages/boot/app-boot/tests/user-patches.spec.ts`, `apps/cli/tests/windows-shell.spec.ts`.

### Sync procedure

For **updating** an existing vendored package (adding a new one is `docs/cookbook/adding-a-vendored-package.md`):

1. In the upstream workspace, note `git rev-parse HEAD` of the relevant submodule.
2. Copy the package's `src/` (and `bin.js`, `README.md`, `LICENSE` if changed) over the vendored directory.
3. Re-apply the local modifications above — or drop them if upstream made them unnecessary — updating the log either way.
4. Update version and commit hash in the manifest table.
5. Run `pnpm install &amp;&amp; pnpm run test &amp;&amp; pnpm run build` at the repo root.

`docs/rescope.md` adds a post-sync step: re-run `pnpm run rescope-vendor --apply`, then the regeneration it prints — `pnpm install` (lockfile), `pnpm run gen-third-party-notices`, `pnpm run verify-translation-pairing --write`.

Two entries in the log are self-marked as transient or non-upstream surface, worth flagging for a codebase report: #7 says "Retire this entry when the enrichment is upstreamed to the fork," and #5 notes the two `tsdown.config.ts` files are "not part of the upstream sync surface." Modification #15 is an already-upstreamed-in-PR-form port, so it may collapse on the next `cordiverse/cordis` sync; #11's index-rebuilding change looks like a genuine upstream bug that has not been sent upstream.</result>

## The vision (from `.agents/notes/proposed/architecture/2026-08-26-agentic-web-desktop-repository-restructuring.md`, 409 lines — the owning plan)

Original dsh = a capable agent runtime behind **one privileged three-column Chat interface**: `ui-layout` owned the browser `root`, there was one "current Session" page-wide, and two aggregate bundles (`dsh-base`, `dsh-web-app`) fused runtime spine + shipped agent + local machine + web platform + every Chat feature.

The target is a **multi-user personal web environment**. Core objects and rules:

- **Space** — the form-factor-neutral *durable layout* (window graph). Explicitly *not* called "Desktop": that names one form factor, while Window/Surface/launcher survive phones, tablets, and native shells. The browser windowed shell is one *renderer* of a Space.
- **Window** — presentation/navigation state only. Closing it never cancels an Agent, deletes a Session, stops a plugin run, or deletes a resource.
- **Surface** — a plugin-contributed launchable renderer (`SurfaceDefinition`: stable id, owning Application, label, icon, launch-state schema/version, default placement). Host publishes the definition; the Client registers a renderer into the keyed `space.window.surface` slot under the same id. Missing/failed/incompatible renderer ⇒ **recovery tile**, never data loss.
- **Application** — a Plugin that registers one stable Application identity plus ≥1 Surface. It is an *installation target* and the subject of Space presets — never a second lifecycle, package kind, or base class.
- **Notes** — the first non-Chat shared application service (`ctx.notes`), proving the shell is not "Chat with movable panels": one authoritative record edited by UI, another Window, and an Agent tool alike.
- **Personal plugins / Plugin Studio** — immutable content-addressed packages, durable installations, preview → approval → atomic promotion → rollback, Surfaces from the approved manifest.
- **Automations** — `ctx.automations` binds typed runtime events to Agents durably (summarize an edited Note, review a promotion) instead of hand-rolled `ctx.on()` + `ctx.agents.create()`.
- **Presets / installation targets** — a Space binds one preset immutably at creation; the preset allowlists Applications and toggles the launcher; one host check at launch resolution rejects `application-unavailable` identically for launcher, Remote, and agent tools.
- Rules throughout: human UI and agent tools consume the *same* Host service; one personal runtime = one user-plugin trust domain; Session logs stay authoritative only for model-visible history (Space geometry and Note bodies live in their own stores); every durable id branded, every executable revision immutable, every mutable aggregate monotonically revisioned.

**Phases 0–9**: 0 freeze names/baselines; 1 split composition + client business runtimes; 2 durable Space + single-Surface reconstruction; 3 explicit Session binding + multi-window Chat; 4 Notes vertical slice + space tools; 5 installation targets + Space presets; 6 authenticated control plane + isolated runners (OIDC, Postgres, runner supervisor, generation fencing — *not started*, and where the product fork on `product/main` begins); 7 durable personal plugins + Plugin Studio; 8 hosted execution + grant enforcement (*not started*); 9 collaboration + multi-host placement (*not started*). Phases 1–4 and 7 are committed; **5 is the uncommitted work-in-progress**. Note that 7 was landed out of order, before 5/6.

## Commit-by-commit

| Commit | Phase | Delivered |
|---|---|---|
| `185c812e17` | — | The planning notes themselves (2374 lines in `.agents/`). |
| `7604ca4b99` (+ fix `6ae2a1dcaa`) | side capability | New `packages/automation/` family: `automation` (Service Definition, branded rule/run records, `automation/dispatched`), `automation-domain` (durable provider over `ctx.storageDomain`, live matching, at-most-once delivery, kill switch), `tool-automation`. ~6.9k lines. |
| `04bc0ebbcb` | **1** | Split `client/runtime` → `runtime` (generic slots/store/stream) + `session-runtime` + `workspace-runtime`; split `ui-layout` → `ui-shell` (owns `root`) + `ui-chat-surface`; `ui-sidebar`→`ui-chat-sidebar`; renamed 830 slot occurrences into `chat.*`; two aggregate bundles → seven (`runtime-core`, `agent-standard`, `local-machine`, `web-platform`, `desktop-shell`, `app-chat`, `app-settings`). 784 files, zero visible change, proven by identical composition tuples and byte-identical aria goldens. |
| `c94893a031` | **2** | New `packages/desktop/` family: `desktop` (Service Definition, brands, `SurfaceDefinition` registry, 6 Remote methods), `desktop-domain` (one-record aggregate per Space over `storageDomain`, per-Space operation tail, `mutationId` idempotency, `expectedRevision` CAS, publish-at-commit, LRU retention), `surface-chat`; plus `client/desktop-runtime` mirror. Slots gained a typed `meta` axis; Chat now renders from a durable Window record. |
| `df02c9230f` | **3** | Biggest client change (~10.5k lines in `packages/client`). `ISessions` lost `current`/`open()`/`clear()`; gained refcounted `acquireView`; `SessionProvider` takes an explicit bundle; per-window state on an `instanceKey` axis; `layout` service deleted. Host gained geometry, `full/floating/minimized`, durable `focusedWindowId`, `updateWindow`/`focusWindow`; `space/changed` slimmed to `{spaceId, revision}`. Shell became a real window manager: titlebars, drag/resize, taskbar with roving tabindex, launcher, narrow-viewport fallback, completion notices, `document.title`. |
| `aa387db6ca` | **4** | New `packages/note/` family (`note`, `note-domain`, `surface-note`, `tool-note`) + `client/ui-note` + `bundle/app-notes`; `tool-desktop` (`space_list`, `space_open_window`, `space_window`). Conflict contract with no silent overwrite (Reload/Overwrite banner). Cascade placement moved host-side. 14.1k lines. |
| `2bb9aeaac1` | **7** | New `packages/plugin-runtime/` — six packages: `plugin-registry`, `plugin-package-store` (`sha256:` content-addressed, staging write + fsync + non-clobbering link), `personal-client-runner`, `surface-plugin-studio`, `ui-plugin-studio`, `tool-plugin-development`; + `bundle/personal-plugin-runtime`. Per-version approval, no standing grant; rollback the sole exception; one aggregate `put` is the commit point; two serialization lanes (`plugins:&lt;id&gt;` / `data:&lt;id&gt;`). 20.6k lines — the largest commit. |

| `bbfe29b60f` | — | **Desktop → Space rename**, 425 files, ~6.1k/6.1k. `packages/desktop/*` → `packages/space/*` (`space`, `space-domain`, `surface-chat`, `tool-space`); `client/desktop-runtime` → `client/space-runtime`; `client/ui-desktop` → `client/ui-shell`; `bundle/desktop-shell` → `bundle/space-shell`; `docs/subsystems/desktop.md` → `space.md`; `DesktopService` → `SpaceService`. |

## New on this branch vs master

Entirely new top-level package families: **`packages/automation/`**, **`packages/note/`**, **`packages/plugin-runtime/`**, **`packages/space/`** (born as `packages/desktop/`). New client packages: `session-runtime`, `workspace-runtime`, `space-runtime`, `ui-chat-surface`, `ui-note`, `ui-shell` (from `ui-desktop`), `ui-plugin-studio`. New bundles: `agent-standard`, `local-machine`, `app-chat`, `app-settings`, `app-notes`, `space-shell`, `personal-plugin-runtime` (plus `runtime-core`/`web-platform` from the `base`/`web-app` split). No new `apps/` (`platform-api` and `runner-supervisor` are phase-6, unstarted). Total across the 11 commits: roughly 72k lines added.

## The ~8k-line uncommitted WIP (201 files, +8066/−1058)

This is **phase 5 plus two follow-on increments**, with three note triplets still untracked. Weight: `packages/space` 2721, `packages/client` 2610, `packages/plugin-runtime` 1371, then docs/tests/snapshots.

1. **Phase 5 proper** (`2026-08-29-application-targets-and-space-presets.md`): `ctx.spaces.registerApplication` with a standing mount per Application (a named empty plugin scope); `SurfaceDefinition.applicationId` required, and `registerSurface` refuses an unregistered Application. New brands `ApplicationId`, `SpacePresetId` in `packages/space/space/src/types.ts`. Space storage bumped to v2 with a required `presetId` (discards prior Spaces); `resolve`/`create` answer a `ResolvedSpace` so `all` stays symbolic. `PersonalPluginRecord.target` is a discriminated union `{kind:'runtime'} | {kind:'application', applicationId}` fixed at first install, `target-mismatch` on disagreement (visible in `plugin-registry/src/index.ts`); unmountable Application ⇒ named `failed` activation with `application-mount` diagnostics. Manifests gained `exports` (display-only). Ships `developer` + `shared` presets, the latter patched in from `app-notes`.
2. **Launcher Space switcher** (`2026-08-30-launcher-space-switcher.md`): a recorded reversal — `SpaceService.list()` goes on the wire; taskbar menu gains a "Switch space" section (8 max, launcher-off Spaces excluded), `switchSpace` re-enforces refusal host-side.
3. **Named launches + hierarchical launcher + context presets** (`2026-08-30-named-launches-and-context-presets.md`): `SurfaceDefinition.launches` (`{id,label,launchState}`), `OpenWindowRequest.launchId` (mutually exclusive with `launchState`, `unknown-launch` rejection), `updateSurfaceLaunches` for async registrants; `Menu.tsx` gained a one-level `submenu` kind (+369 lines) with `type?: never` nesting guard; Chat declares one launch per agent preset (`new-&lt;presetId&gt;`); `agent-presets` gained ordered `defaults` rules matched by workspace path prefix and `resolveFor()`; `space-domain` gained `presetRules` matched against a new `ClientFormFactor` hint computed once per page in the new `packages/client/space-runtime/src/client/form-factor.ts`; and `standard` is now a named, e2e-proven vanilla-dsh compatibility preset.

New untracked tests confirm the scope: `apps/web/tests/space-launches.e2e.ts`, `space-switcher.e2e.ts`, `space-shared-preset.e2e.ts`, `space-application-outage.e2e.ts` (+overlay), `packages/space/space-domain/tests/applications.spec.ts` and `presets.spec.ts`, `plugin-registry/tests/installation-target.spec.ts`, `agent-presets/tests/defaults.spec.ts`, and headless snapshots `plugin-target/`, `plugin-target-restart/`, `shared-space/`, `space-launch/`.</result>

<summary>Agent "Review plan: design elegance" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>## Overall verdict

Well-argued and unusually disciplined for a split plan — but **not yet elegant by this repo's own standards**. Two of the three ownership clauses are principled; the third (fork boot) is a wart that, as written, *cannot even deliver the divergence it claims*. The dual manifest key is spin measured against this repo's own naming contract. And "this is the complete list; nothing else moves" is falsified by four concrete dependency edges in the tree today, plus ~102 active Agent Notes. The bones are good; three sections need rework before this is landable.

What genuinely earns praise: the **"Not renamed" list** (`ctx.*` keys, slot namespaces, `SessionEventMap`, `SESSION_FORMAT_VERSION`, `@deepseek-ai/cordis*`) is the strongest part of the note — correct calls for a single shared in-process Cordis runtime, with the right reason given. Workspace-globbing the submodule so pre-release renames surface as compile errors in one pass is a real design choice, not a convenience. Migration step 2 (boot today's product entirely from submodule packages, zero moves) makes the skeleton falsifiable before any code moves. The npm-versions alternative is *deferred with a stated condition*, not hand-waved. `verify no diff under dsh/` is the right mechanical enforcement of read-only.
